Table 1. Account Parameters and Statistics
 
In the 2009 Joint Proposal in Gas Rate Case 08–G–0609, the Company was instructed to 
establish, beginning in January 1, 2010, two subaccounts that would identify metallic and non–
metallic services and prospectively classify and record gas services in these subaccounts. The 
Company subsequently established these subaccounts and in addition retroactively identified, by 
these material types, plant transactions (i.e. additions, retirements, transfers, and adjustments) 
starting in 2004. The net salvage history, however, could not be retroactively identified and 
therefore includes only transactions recorded since 2011. The service life analysis and parameter 
estimation performed in this study is therefore based on 2004–2018 observation bands by 
material type while the net salvage analysis and future net salvage estimation is based upon a 
2011–2018 history of retirements, gross salvage, and cost of removal by material type. 
Service Life Analysis 
Although metallic pipe was the dominant type of service in the past, plastic pipe is now 
dominant because it is easier to install, is more resistant to corrosive soil conditions, and costs 
less per unit than metallic pipe.  
Forces of retirement acting upon metallic services include both physical and electrochemical 
corrosion and damage from frost, vibrations, trenching, and excavations. Factors such as urban 
renewal projects, condemnation, and abandonment of residential structures also tend to shorten 
the service life of services. 
The statistical service life analysis for this subaccount indicates average service lives exceeding 
80 years. Although such long average service lives are conceivable for this account because of 
Exhibit___(KAK-3)
Page 240 of 441


--- Page 310 ---
  
NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS  
Distribution Plant    
Non-Leak-Prone 
Account: 380.10  Services - Metallic 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 2 of 3 
 
very old metallic pipes that are still in service, it is likely that increasing use of plastic will 
steadily erode any remaining metallic advantage relative to plastic and eventually lead to 
substantial metallic retirements and declining average service lives of metallic pipes. It is also 
conceivable that the retrospective identification of plant transactions by material type conducted 
by the Company was less than perfect and might explain the extreme average service lives 
indicated in the statistical service life analysis. Another complicating factor is the relatively short 
observation period used in the statistical analysis. 
Investment of $37,682,180 in leak–prone pipes at December 31, 2018 was identified by the 
Company and transferred to a separate subaccount, 380.30, for proactive and accelerated 
replacement. 
Life Estimation 
In its "Reactive Main Replacement Program", the Company expects to replace smaller sections 
of main segments and associated services that are identified during leak surveys and cannot be 
repaired by simple leak clamps. This program will allow Field Operations to make quick 
decisions on replacing actively deteriorating segments of pipe without Gas Engineering approval. 
The program covers the Company's inventory of (i) non–cathodically protected steel pipe (coated 
or bare), (ii) cast and wrought iron pipe, and (iii) pre–1985 Dupont Aldyl–A plastic pipe.  
Also, in its "Reactive Atmospheric Corrosion & Plastic Fusions" program, the Company will 
address the heightened industrywide awareness of potential risks attributable to atmospheric 
corrosion and failed plastic fusions. Atmospheric corrosion inspection of outside and inside 
services will include visual inspection of: i) the service riser and soil–to–air interface, ii) the 
service piping through the outlet of the meter, regulator, and fittings, and iii) the wall penetration 
or point of entry. The Company will proactively replace any services failing inspection. 
The "Reactive Service Replacement" program will replace leaking or damaged services and 
retire and seal abandoned services due to inactivity or demolition request, customer driven 
relocations, and other substandard conditions. 
Without a clear indication at this time of the extent to which these observations and initiatives 
will affect the future measured average service life of this account, Foster Associates is 
recommending retention of the currently approved parameters for this account. 
Net Salvage Analysis 
The adjusted net salvage analysis for this account shows a full band realized net salvage rate of -
45.1 percent and a 5–year rolling band ending in 2018 of -47.5 percent. 
Net Salvage Estimation 
No gross salvage is expected from retired pipe. Cost associated with the retirement or removal of 
pipe, however, can be substantial. Although the nature of the work needed to retire or remove 
pipe is situational, it typically involves a number of costly activities such as conditioning the site 
(i.e. obtaining permits, setting up signs and checking for the presence of other shared utilities, a 
Exhibit___(KAK-3)
Page 241 of 441


--- Page 311 ---
  
NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS  
Distribution Plant    
Non-Leak-Prone 
Account: 380.10  Services - Metallic 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 3 of 3 
 
process called "Dig Safe"), stopping the flow of gas and purging the pipe of all remaining gas, 
checking for contaminants, digging, excavating, cutting, capping, backfilling with grout, 
abandoning in place or removing pipe for safe and environmentally acceptable disposal since 
used pipe is always considered contaminated. Site restoration is invariably mandatory. 
Equipment such as backhoes, excavators, dump trucks and "suck–down trucks" which extract the 
remaining gas out of the line and into adjacent lines are almost invariably required at the site. 
Coordination with other operations personnel is often required to adjust station pressures, operate 
valves, redirect the flow of gas in order to reduce pressures and vent as little gas as possible to 
the atmosphere (for environmental reasons). All removed pipe requires testing for PCBs and the 
positive results dictate specific abandonment procedures. The cost incurred in retiring, replacing, 
or abandoning a service is, therefore, labor intensive, sensitive to inflation, and substantial. 
The development of a net salvage rate for distribution metallic services should also recognize 
that removal expense incurred in any given year is largely independent of the age of the plant 
retired from service. Moreover, because future removal expense is sensitive to inflation, it is 
necessary to consider the timing of future retirements and the age of the plant when it will most 
likely be retired from service.  Removal expense incurred in current dollars and applied to 
relatively older dollars of investment produces the high negative net salvage rates observed for 
this subaccount. 
Both the historical realized net salvage and the discussion above support the expectation that 
substantial cost of retiring and removal will be incurred in the future. Based on these 
observations, Foster Associates is recommending a revision to a -25 percent future net salvage 
rate to move the expectation in the direction of the observed net salvage rates.

NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS  
Distribution Plant    
Non-Leak-Prone 
Account: 380.20  Services - Plastic 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 1 of 3 
 
Description 
This account includes the installed cost of plastic distribution services. A complete service 
begins with the connection on the main and extends to but does not include the connection with 
the customer's meter.  A stub service extends from the main to the property line, or the curb stop. 
Services which have been used but have become inactive are retired from utility plant in service 
immediately if there is no prospect for reuse, and, in any event, are retired by the end of the 
second year following that during which the service became inactive unless reused in the interim. 
Account statistics and current and proposed parameters are shown in Table 1 below. 
 
Current

Table 1. Account Parameters and Statistics
 
In the 2009 Joint Proposal in Gas Rate Case 08–G–0609, the Company was instructed to 
establish, beginning in January 1, 2010, two subaccounts that would identify metallic and non-
metallic services and prospectively classify and record gas services in these subaccounts. The 
Company subsequently established these subaccounts and in addition retroactively identified, by 
these material types, plant transactions (i.e. additions, retirements, transfers, and adjustments) 
starting in 2004. The net salvage history, however, could not be retroactively identified and 
therefore includes only transactions recorded since 2011. The service life analysis and parameter 
estimation performed in this study is therefore based on 2004–2018 observation bands by 
material type while the net salvage analysis and future net salvage estimation is based upon a 
2011–2018 history of retirements, gross salvage, and cost of removal by material type. 
Service Life Analysis 
Although metallic pipe was the dominant material type in the past, plastic pipe is now the 
material of choice because it is lighter, flexible and easier to install. Plastic is also more resistant 
to corrosive soil conditions and costs less per unit to install than metallic pipe. Forces of 
retirement acting upon plastic services include degradation and damage from frost, vibrations, 
trenching, and excavations. Factors such as urban renewal projects, condemnation, and 
abandonment of residential structures also tend to shorten the service life of services. 
The statistical service life analysis for this subaccount indicates service lives in the upper 40s to 
mid–50s for broader bands with lower censoring and conformance indexes.

NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS

Account: 380.20  Services - Plastic 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 2 of 3 
 
Investment of $12,010,671 in leak–prone plastic services pipes at December 31, 2018 was 
identified by the Company and transferred to a separate subaccount, 380.40, for proactive and 
accelerated replacement. 
Life Estimation 
In its "Reactive Main Replacement Program", the Company expects to replace smaller sections 
of main segments and associated services that are identified during leak surveys and cannot be 
repaired by simple leak clamps. This program will allow Field Operations to make quick 
decisions on replacing actively deteriorating segments of pipe without Gas Engineering approval. 
The program covers the Company's inventory of (i) non–cathodically protected steel pipe (coated 
or bare), (ii) cast and wrought iron pipe, and (iii) pre–1985 Dupont Aldyl–A plastic pipe. 
Also, in its "Reactive Atmospheric Corrosion & Plastic Fusions" program, the Company will 
address the heightened industrywide awareness of potential risks attributable to atmospheric 
corrosion and failed plastic fusions. Atmospheric corrosion inspection of outside and inside 
services will include visual inspection of: i) the service riser and ground–to–air interface, ii) the 
service piping through the outlet of the meter, regulator, and fittings, and iii) the wall penetration 
or point of entry. The Company will proactive replace any services failing inspection. 
The "Reactive Service Replacement" program will replace leaking or damaged services and 
retire and seal abandoned services due to inactivity or demolition request, customer driven 
relocations, and other substandard conditions. 
Without a clear indication at this time of the extent to which these observations and initiatives 
will affect the future measured average service life of this account, Foster Associates is basing its 
estimation on the indications from the statistical service life analysis which generally support a 
shorter projection life than currently prescribed. Based on that observation, a 50–S2 projection 
life-curve is recommended for this account. 
Net Salvage Analysis 
The net salvage analysis for this account shows a -40.7 percent full band realized net salvage 
rate. The five–year rolling band ending in 2018 shows a -32.5 percent realized net salvage rate 
Net Salvage Estimation 
No gross salvage is expected from retired pipe. Cost associated with the retirement or removal of 
pipe, however, can be substantial. Although the nature of the work needed to retire or remove 
pipe is situational, it typically involves a number of costly activities such as conditioning the site 
(i.e. obtaining permits, setting up signs and checking for the presence of other shared utilities, a 
process called "Dig Safe"), stopping the flow of gas and purging the pipe of all remaining gas, 
checking for contaminants, digging, excavating, cutting, capping, backfilling with grout, 
abandoning in place or removing pipe for safe and environmentally acceptable disposal since 
used pipe is always considered contaminated. Site restoration is invariably mandatory. 
Equipment such as backhoes, excavators, dump trucks and "suck-down trucks" which suck the 
remaining gas out of the line and into adjacent lines are almost invariably required at the site. 
Exhibit___(KAK-3)
Page 262 of 441


--- Page 332 ---
  
NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS  
Distribution Plant    
Non-Leak-Prone 
Account: 380.20  Services - Plastic 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 3 of 3 
 
Coordination with other operations personnel is often required to adjust station pressures, operate 
valves, redirect the flow of gas in order to reduce pressures and vent as little gas as possible to 
the atmosphere (for environmental reasons). All removed pipe requires testing for PCBs and the 
positive results dictate specific abandonment procedures. The cost incurred in retiring, replacing, 
or abandoning a service is, therefore, labor intensive, sensitive to inflation, and substantial. 
The development of a net salvage rate for distribution plastic services should also recognize that 
removal expense incurred in any given year is largely independent of the age of the plant retired 
from service. Moreover, because future removal expense is sensitive to inflation, it is necessary 
to consider the timing of future retirements and the age of the plant when it will most likely be 
retired from service.  Removal expense incurred in current dollars and applied to relatively older 
dollars of investment produces the high negative net salvage rates observed for this subaccount. 
Both the historical realized net salvage and the discussion above support the expectation that 
substantial cost of retiring and removal will be incurred in the future. Based on these 
observations, Foster Associates is recommending a revision from the currently approved -10 
percent to a -25 percent future net salvage rate for this account.

--- Page 350 ---
  
NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS  
Distribution Plant    
Non-Leak-Prone 
Account: 381.00  Meters 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 1 of 1 
 
Description 
This account includes the cost of meters or devices (whether in service or held in reserve) used in 
measuring the quantity of gas delivered to users. The Company conducts a mandated annual 
sampling of meters to check for accuracy. Account statistics and current and proposed 
parameters are shown in Table 1 below. 
 
Current
Proposed
PLife-Curve
33-R2.5
30-R2.5
Future Net Sal. Rate
-5.0%
0.0%
Realized Net Sal. Rate
-4.0%
Average Age (yrs.)
14.1
Derived Additions
$124,822,931
Plant Retirements
$28,956,024
Percent Retired 
23.2%
Plant Balance
$95,866,907
Table 1. Account Parameters and Statistics
 
Service Life Analysis 
When censoring and conformance indexes are low, the actuarial service life analysis conducted 
for this account indicates average service lives between the high–20s for more recent bands and 
the mid–30s for broader bands.  
Life Estimation 
The Company is required by state code to conduct statistical sampling to check meter accuracy. 
The most recent calendar year results revealed a high out–of–tolerance rate for a large subset of 
Sensus R275TC meters. The Company has started replacing this subset of meters, about 33,000, 
with an expected completion date of fiscal year 2026. Additionally, the Company is planning to 
implement an Advanced Meter Infrastructure (AMI) solution to modernize its gas and electric 
system. Although electric meters are expected to be affected more than gas meters by AMI 
infrastructure, it is likely that about 10 percent (64,000 meters) of the total gas meter population 
will require replacement during AMI implementation. These programs are expected to increase 
retirement activity and possibly result in a shorter measured average service life for this account. 
Based on the more recent statistical service life indications and the expected increase in 
retirement activity, a revision to a 30–year projection life is recommended for this account. 
Net Salvage Analysis 
Although the adjusted net salvage analysis for this account shows a -4 percent overall realized 
net salvage rate, more recent five–year rolling bands show rates around 0 percent.  
Net Salvage Estimation 
Based on these observations, Foster Associates is recommending a 0 percent future net salvage 
rate for this account.

NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS  
Distribution Plant    
Non-Leak-Prone 
Account: 382.00  Meter Installations 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 1 of 1 
 
Description 
This account includes the cost of labour, materials such as meter bars, and expenses incurred in 
connection with the original installation of residential meters. Account statistics and current and 
proposed parameters are shown in Table 1 below. 
 
Current
Proposed
PLife-Curve
50-R1
30-R2.5
Future Net Sal. Rate
-50.0%
-50.0%
Realized Net Sal. Rate
-63.6%

$115,142,867
Plant Retirements
$15,832,430

13.8%
Plant Balance
$99,310,437
Table 1. Account Parameters and Statistics
 
Service Life Analysis 
The actuarial service life analysis conducted for this account indicates average service lives 
between the low–40s to mid–40s for lower–censored broader bands. 
Life Estimation 
Foster Associates was informed that the accounting for retirements in this account is under 
review because it may not be in compliance with a FERC authorization received in 1997. 
According to the authorization, a meter installation would be retired when a meter is moved or 
retired. Given that meters are moved frequently without being retired, the average service life of 
meter installations would not be expected to exceed the average service life of meters. Pending 
results from the Company's review, Foster Associates was requested by the Company to apply 
the service life indications for Account 381.00 – Meters to this account as the upper limit of the 
service life expectation. A 30–R2.5 projection life–curve is, therefore, recommended for this 
account. 
Net Salvage Analysis 
Net salvage reported in this account is due to the cost of retiring meters. The adjusted net salvage 
analysis shows more negative net salvage in recent years and a -63.6 percent overall realized net 
salvage. The five–year rolling band ending in 2018 shows a -66.7 percent realized net salvage 
rate.  
Net Salvage Estimation 
Based on these observations, Foster Associates is recommending a retention of the currently 
prescribed -50 percent future net salvage rate.

Service Life and Net Salvage Analysis and Estimation

salvage parameters pending the review.

Service Life and Net Salvage Analysis and Estimation

NIAGARA MOHAWK POWER CORPORATION - GAS  
Distribution Plant    
Non-Leak-Prone 
Account: 385.00  Indust. Meas. and Reg. Sta. Equip. 
 
Account Analysis and Parameter Estimation 
        
 
Page 1 of 1 
 
Description 
This account includes the cost of gauges, RTUs, piping, reliefs, regulators and other equipment 
used in measuring or regulating gas in connection with gas distribution operations. Account 
statistics and current and proposed parameters are shown in Table 1 below. 
 
Current
Proposed
PLife-Curve

$5,101,311
Table 1. Account Parameters and Statistics
 
Service Life Analysis 
Only $62,917 of investment has been retired from this account since 1997, the earliest year in the 
observation band. The average plant balance over that period was more than $4.6M. The derived 
retirement rates on which the statistical service life analysis is based are insubstantial and tend to 
generate negative estimators of the hazard function. Broader observation bands are highly 
censored and do not provided reliable service life estimates. 
Life Estimation 
Based on these observations and absent any other evidence, Foster Associates is recommending a 
retention of the currently prescribed 40–R5 projection life-curve. 
Net Salvage Analysis 
The adjusted net salvage analysis for this account shows a -5.1 percent realized net salvage rate 
over the period for which retirements were reported. Although a $50,000 amount of gross 
salvage was reported in 2000, it appears to be unrelated to subsequent retirements.   
Net Salvage Estimation 
Based on the net salvage analysis, Foster Associates is recommending a -5 percent future net 
salvage rate for this account.

Service Life Analysis 
No retirements have been recorded in this account since 2005, the earliest year in the observation 
band. Because no statistical service life analysis could be conducted, and without any evidence to 
the contrary, Foster Associates is recommending a retention of the currently prescribed service 
life and net salvage parameters. 
Life Estimation 
Absent any retirement experience to conduct a service life analysis, Foster Associates is 
recommending a retention of the currently prescribed 55–L0.5 projection life-curve. 
Net Salvage Analysis 
The lack of retirement activity prevented a net salvage analysis for this account. 
Net Salvage Estimation 
Absent any retirement experience to conduct a net salvage analysis, Foster Associates is 
recommending a retention of the currently prescribed 0 percent future net salvage rate.
